Let's implement a combination:
1. Generate candidate sequences and evaluate with a fast scorer using FFT (or direct for small). Keep best.
2. For each top candidate, run local gradient descent (soft-max multiplicative) for some iterations.
3. Run coordinate factor hill climbing on best to polish.
4. Use evaluate_sequence on top candidates to confirm exact score (safe) and return best exact.

Need ensure final sequence list `[float(x) for x in best]`, nonnegative, finite, length reasonable. If gradient produces tiny negatives from projection, clip.

Let's detail fast scorer:
```
def fast_score(a, fft_cache=None):
    a = np.asarray(a,float)
    n = len(a)
    if n==0: return inf
    # optional cap? no, use actual values; score invariant. But to avoid huge, normalize
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return inf
    # To compare with evaluate_sequence, we can scale to sum1; score=2n*max(np.convolve(p,p))
    p = a/s
    # Use FFT size N=1
    N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
    f = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(f*f, N)[:2*n-1]
    b = np.maximum(b,0) # numerical
    return 2*n*max(b)
```
Because normalization sum1, score=2nM. This matches evaluate_sequence exactly up to numeric. We can use direct for n≤512 for validation.

FFT cache per n: precompute N, maybe use `np.fft.rfft` each pass.

```
def gradient_step(p, b, it):
    M=b.max()
    # weights: top K or exponential
```

Let's implement gradient iteration carefully.

```
def compute_conv(a, N):
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(f*f, N)
    return b[:2*len(a)-1]
```
For normalized p, values small; numerical negative small set to 0.

Active gradient:
```
def make_subgradient(p, b, top_k=64, beta_factor=50.0):
    n = len(p)
    L = len(b)
    # if all b zero? return None
    # choose top indices
    # Use np.argpartition for top_k
    if top_k >= L:
        idx = np.argsort(b)[::-1]
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(b, -top_k)[-top_k:]
        idx = idx[np.argsort(b[idx])[::-1]]
    vals = b[idx]
    M = vals[-1]
    # weights: use exponential over vals, beta = beta_factor / max(M, tiny)
    tiny = max(1e-300, M*1e-12)
    beta = beta_factor / max(M, tiny)
    # If vals all equal, weights uniform
    z = np.exp(beta*(vals - M)) # z[-1]=1, others <=1
    if not np.isfinite(z).sum():
        z = np.ones_like(vals)
    zsum = z.sum()
    if zsum <= 0: z = np.ones_like(vals); zsum = len(z)
    w = z / zsum
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # To reduce loops, combine weights.
    for kk, ww in zip(idx, w):
        if ww <= 0: continue
        k = int(kk)
        lo = k - n + 1
        if lo < 0: lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n: hi = n - 1
        if lo <= hi:
            # segment length hi-lo+1, p slice reversed
            # p indices from k-hi down to k-lo
            g[lo:hi+1] += ww * p[k-hi : k-lo : -1]
    # derivative includes factor 2
    g *= 2.0
    mean = np.dot(p, g)
    d = g - mean
    return d, np.max(np.abs(d))
```
Note weights normalized sum1; if active plateau huge and top_k n? For central binomial, b0..b_{n-1}=1, top_k picks 64 average. It will reduce a random subset of prefix constraints, maybe not global. Could bias to include oldest and newest? Maybe use active set as all with b >= M - tol if count not too large. For central, count n (too many); but gradient average over all plateau could be computed as convolution of uniform plateau? We can choose top_k=256 or min(n, 512). More constraints gives smoother gradient but loops cost. K=128 maybe okay. We can also include boundary indices 0 and L-1 to handle endpoints: add them if not in top.

```
def gradient_search(p, N, lr_candidates, score):
    b = compute_conv(p,N)
    d, norm = make_subgradient(p,b,...)
    if norm < eps: return p, score, True
    p_scale = np.max(p)
    # choose base lr: maybe 1.0 / norm? We'll test multipliers.
    # Since p components sum1; want step*d ~ O(p.mean()). base = 0.05 * p.mean()/norm?
    base = 0.1 * (1.0/n) / norm
    # But if norm very small, base huge. cap.
    base = min(max(base, 1e-6), 1.0)
    # Try multipliers [0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0]
    best_p=p; best_score=score
    for mult in mults:
       step=base*mult
       q=p - step*d
       q=np.maximum(q,0)
       s=q.sum()
       if s < 1e-12: continue
       q/=s
       sc=fast_score(q,N)
       if sc < best_score:
           best_score=sc; best_p=q
    return best_p,best_score,False
```

```
def random_factor_polish(a, N, iterations, rng):
    score = fast_score(a,N)
    for _ in range(iterations):
       j = int(rng.integers(n))
       test_factors = [0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0^? 1.3,2,5]
       old = a[j]
       best = score
       for f in factors:
           if old==0: # try absolute values based on mean
           a[j]=old*f
           sc=fast_score(a,N)
           if sc<best: best=sc; bestf=f
       a[j]=old*bestf
       a[j]=old # if no improve
       if improve: score=best; improve_count++
       else: if no improve many, break? 
```

```
def random_smooth_perturb(base, rng, strength):
    p = base.copy()
    # add block multiplicative noise: split into blocks of width w, multiply exp(N(0,strength))
    # maybe also add low-frequency sinusoidal factors
    p = np.maximum(p,0)
    norm = p.sum()
    if norm>0: p/=norm
    return p
```

```
def run_gradient_local(p, n, N, time_limit, rng, beta_factor=50.0, top_k=128):
    start = time.monotonic()
    score = fast_score(p,N)
    no_improve = 0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit:
        b = conv(p,N)
        # use b from fast_score? fast_score computed b? To avoid recompute, modify fast_score to return score,b? We can have `conv_and_score`.
        M = b.max()
        if M == 0: break
        d,norm = subgradient(...)
        if norm < 1e-18: break
        base = 0.05 * (1.0/n) / norm
        base = min(max(base, 1e-8), 2.0)
        improved=False
        # try multiplicative steps
        for factor in (0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
            lr = base*factor
            q = p * np.exp(-lr*d)
            q = np.maximum(q,0)
            s=q.sum()
            if s < 1e-12: continue
            q/=s
            sc = fast_score(q,N)
            if sc < score - 1e-15:
                p=q; score=sc; improved=True; break
        if not improved:
            # try additive with smaller base maybe
            for step in (0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0):
                 q = p - step*d
                 ...
            if improved: ...
            else:
                 # random nudge
                 # occasionally perturb one random block
        it+=1
        if score < best_global: update
    return p,score
```

Compute gradient with FFT:
```
def soft_gradient_fft(p, b, n, M_fft, beta_factor=10.0):
    L=2*n-1
    M=b.max()
    beta=beta_factor / max(M, 1e-300)
    z=np.exp(beta*(b-M))
    s=z.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s==0: z=np.zeros(L); z[np.argmax(b)]=1; s=1
    w=z/s
    wr=w[::-1]
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p, M_fft)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(wr, M_fft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fw, M_fft)
    # c length M_fft, take indices n-1 .. 2n-2
    seg = c[n-1:2*n-1]  # length n, indices n-1..2n-2 inclusive? stop 2n-1 gives up to 2n-2 yes.
    A=seg[::-1]
    g=2*A
    # numerical small negative? A nonnegative theoretically; clip?
    g=np.maximum(g,0) # derivatives nonnegative? Yes w,p nonneg. But after FFT numerical, clip small.
    mean=np.dot(p,g)
    d=g-mean
    return d, np.max(np.abs(d))
```
Check c index stop: L=2n-1, max index L-1=2n-2. `c[n-1:2*n-1]` length 2n-1 - (n-1)=n. Good.

M_fft should be ≥ n+L-1 = 3n-2. Choose power of 2. For n=16384, 3n-2=49150, M_fft=65536. Good.

Can we use this gradient for all iters? It is more robust. Then no topK loops. Time maybe acceptable. Let's use for local search; if too slow, topK fallback not needed.

Line search with soft gradient:
```
def local_gradient_search(p, n, time_limit, rng, beta_factor=10.0):
    N = next_power2(3*n-2)
    # also use N for b? b via FFT size N (3n-2) yields enough; conv length 2n-1 okay.
    score,b = conv_and_score(p,n,N) # F^2 length N gives b
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
    it=0; no_improve=0; sigma=0.1
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time < time_limit:
        if it % 100 ==0: check time
        d,norm = soft_gradient_fft(p,b,n,N,beta_factor)
        if norm < 1e-16: break/no improve
        # base step: want exponent max around 0.05-1. Since norm=max|d|, exponent max = lr*norm. Choose lr candidates by exponent target, not base.
        # Multiplicative update q = p * exp(-alpha * d / norm?) If we scale by norm, exponent targets alpha.
        d_unit = d / norm
        # Try alpha in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0]
        improved=False
        for alpha in alphas:
             q = p * np.exp(-alpha*d_unit)
             q=max; s=sum; if s too small skip; q/=s
             sc,b_trial=conv_and_score(q,n,N)
             if sc < score - 1e-15:
                 p=q; score=sc; b=b_trial; improved=True; best...
                 break
        if improved: no_improve=0
        else:
             # try additive with d_unit
             for alpha in alphas:
                 q = p - alpha*d_unit
                 ...
        if not improved:
             no_improve +=1
             if no_improve > 500:
                 # random block perturbation around p, maybe anneal
                 q = block_perturb(p, rng, n, width_factor=..., sigma=0.2)
                 sc,b_trial=...
                 # accept if not much worse than best (anneal)
                 if sc < best_score * (1 + 0.05): p=q; score=sc; b=b_trial
                 no_improve=0
        it +=1
        if score < best_score: update best
    return best_p,best_score
```

`conv_and_score` for normalized p with N size M_fft:
```
def conv_and_score(p,n,N):
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
    b=np.maximum(b,0)
    M=np.max(b)
    return 2*n*M, b
```

Block perturb function:
```
def block_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.2, min_frac=1/200, max_frac=1/4):
    n=len(p); q=p.copy()
    widths = rng.integers(max(1,n*min_frac), max(2,n//4))
    for _ in range(rng.integers(1,4)):
       start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
       q[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength))
       width = ...
    q=np.maximum(q,0); s=q.sum(); if s>0 q/=s
    return q
```
Preserve positivity.

```
def central_binomial_seq(n):
    a=np.ones(n)
    for i in range(1,n):
        a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    return a
```

```
def block_hill_climb(a,n,deadline,seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    N=get_fft_size(n)
    score,b=conv_score(a,n,N)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    no_improve=0; sigma=0.15
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       q=block_perturb(best,rng,sigma)
       sc,_=conv_score(q,n,N)
       if sc < best_score:
          best=q; best_score=sc; no_improve=0; if no_improve%10==0 decrease sigma? 
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve % 100 ==0:
              sigma *= 1.2 if no_improve<2000 else 0.8? We want explore more if stuck: increase sigma up to1.0.
       if no_improve > 5000: break
    return best,best_score
```

Implementation details:
```
def get_fft_size(n):
    m = max(1, 3*n - 2)
    size=1
    while size < m: size <<=1
    return size
```
For small n, size maybe 8.

`conv_score`:
```
def conv_score(p, n, N):
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)
    b = b[:2*n-1]
    # Clip tiny negative
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    M = float(np.max(b))
    return float(2.0 * n * M), b
```
If p sum !=1, score formula wrong? We call with normalized p. Ensure.

`soft_gradient`:
```
def soft_gradient(p,b,n,N,beta_factor=10.0):
    L=2*n-1
    M=float(np.max(b))
    if M <= 0: return np.zeros(n),0.0
    beta = beta_factor / M
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <=0: beta=1.0
    # To prevent overflow, beta*(b-M) <=0; min maybe -inf. Use np.errstate
    z = np.exp(beta * (b - M))
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        z = np.zeros(L); z[int(np.argmax(b))]=1.0; s=1.0
    w = z / s
    wr = w[::-1]
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(wr, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, N)
    seg = c[n-1:2*n-1]
    A = seg[::-1]
    g = 2.0 * A
    # Remove imaginary? c real
    # Clip small negative due to roundoff
    g = np.where(g > 0.0, g, 0.0)
    mean = float(np.dot(p, g))
    d = g - mean
    norm = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    return d, norm
```
Check `c[:2*n-1]` indices: c length N≥3n-2; max index needed 2n-2, stop 2n-1. Good. For n=1? L=1, index n-1=0, stop1 length1; c[0]=p0*w0; A=p0; g=2p0; mean=2p0^2? d? n=1 trivial. Our n large.

`local_search`:
```
def local_search(p,n,budget_s,seed,rng,beta_factor=10.0):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=max(start, start+max(0,budget_s))
    N=get_fft_size(n)
    score,b=conv_score(p,n,N)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score
    it=0; no_improve=0; alpha_current=0.1
    alphas=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        d,norm=soft_gradient(p,b,n,N,beta_factor)
        if norm < 1e-18:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 200: break
        else:
            du = d / norm
            improved=False
            # increasing alphas; current alpha first?
            # Try around current then extremes
            order = [alpha_current] + [a for a in alphas if a != alpha_current]
            for alpha in order:
                q = p * np.exp(-alpha * du)
                q = np.where(q > 0.0, q, 0.0)
                s = float(np.sum(q))
                if s <= 1e-12: continue
                q = q / s
                sc,b2 = conv_score(q,n,N)
                if sc < score - 1e-15:
                    p=q; score=sc; b=b2; improved=True; alpha_current=alpha; break
            if not improved:
                # try additive maybe with scaled du
                for alpha in (0.05,0.2,0.8,2.0):
                    q = p - alpha * du
                    q = np.where(q > 0.0, q, 0.0)
                    s = float(np.sum(q))
                    if s <= 1e-12: continue
                    q /= s
                    sc,b2=conv_score(q,n,N)
                    if sc < score - 1e-15:
                       p=q; score=sc; b=b2; improved=True; break
            if improved:
                no_improve=0
                if score < best_score:
                    best=p.copy(); best_score=score
            else:
                no_improve +=1
                if no_improve % 200 ==0:
                    # random block perturbation with modest, accept if within 10% of best? 
                    q = block_perturb(best, rng, 0.3)
                    sc,b2 = conv_score(q,n,N)
                    if sc < best_score + 0.02 * max(1.0, best_score):
                        p=q; score=sc; b=b2
                    # If still no improve and no_improve>1000, break
                    if no_improve > 2000:
                        break
        it +=1
        if it % 500 == 0:
            # print progress maybe
            pass
    # final maybe block perturb best a bit
    return best,best_score
```
The random perturbation accepts worse than current but within 2% of best, may climb. Might be okay. But if it climbs, score may be > best, local may come back. To prevent losing time, maybe only accept if sc <= best_score (not worse). Use no_improve counter for random with small sigma that could find better. If no better in 2000, break.

Print statements: use sparingly to avoid huge. At start/end of local and final.

But `alpha_current` if improvement with alpha, next order tries same first. Good.

Could optimize by not trying all alphas if first small fails; but okay.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(a*a)_linear / (sum a)^2.

The program uses:
- strong analytic initializers (central-binomial / one-sided sqrt-singular),
- random block/trend perturbations,
- a smooth soft-max gradient local search with FFT line searches,
- a final block hill-climb polish,
- exact scored validation via the provided evaluate_sequence().
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def clean_sequence(p):
    """Return a finite, non-negative, capped, sum-normalized 1D float array."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1 or p.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    p = np.minimum(p, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def next_power_two(x):
    """Smallest power of two >= x."""
    y = max(1, int(x))
    p = 1
    while p < y:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def get_fft_size(n):
    """FFT size large enough for both p*p and cross-correlation gradients."""
    return next_power_two(3 * max(1, n) - 2)


def central_binomial_sequence(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-x)^(-1/2):
        p_0 = 1, p_k = p_{k-1} * (2k-1) / (2k).
    This gives exact first-half flat convolution for the truncated sequence.
    """
    a = np.ones(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(1, a.size):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * (2 * i - 1) / (2 * i)
    return a


def trend_sequence(base, rng, strength):
    """Perturb base by a low-frequency exponential trend."""
    n = base.size
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n - 1)
    q = base * np.exp(strength * rng.normal(0.0, 0.35) * (x - 0.5))
    return clean_sequence(q)


def block_perturb(base, rng, strength=0.25):
    """Random non-negative block multiplicative perturbation."""
    n = base.size
    q = base.copy()
    if n <= 0:
        return q
    min_w = max(1, n // 256)
    max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 4)
    blocks = int(rng.integers(1, 5))
    for _ in range(blocks):
        w = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
        if w >= n:
            w = n
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + w)
        q[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength))
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
    return clean_sequence(q)


def two_block_sequence(n, second_fraction=0.5, ratio=2.0):
    """Two equal half blocks with amplitude ratio 2:1 (known good family)."""
    a = np.ones(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    cut = max(1, min(n - 1, int(round(a.size * second_fraction))))
    a[:cut] = ratio
    a[cut:] = 1.0
    return clean_sequence(a)


def conv_score(p, n, N):
    """
    Fast score for normalized p (sum=1) using FFT.
    Returns (score, convolution).
    """
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    b = b[:L]
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    M = float(np.max(b))
    score = float(2.0 * n * M)
    return score, b


def soft_max_gradient(p, b, n, N, beta_factor=10.0):
    """
    Soft-max weighted subgradient of max(conv(p,p)).
    Returns d = g - mean_p(g) and ||d||_inf, where g is the gradient.
    """
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = float(np.max(b))
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0

    beta = beta_factor / M
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <= 0.0:
        beta = 1.0

    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        z = np.exp(beta * (b - M))
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        z = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        z[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0

    w = z / s
    wr = w[::-1]

    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(wr, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, N)

    # A_j = sum_k w_k p_{k-j}; appears as reversed slice of the convolution p * w[::-1].
    seg = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    A = seg[::-1]
    g = 2.0 * A
    g = np.where(g > 0.0, g, 0.0)

    mean = float(np.dot(p, g))
    d = g - mean
    norm = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if not np.isfinite(norm):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    return d, norm


def local_gradient_search(p, n, budget_s, seed, beta_factor=10.0):
    """
    Smooth local search on the simplex. Multiplicative updates with FFT line
    search, plus occasional random re-seeding to escape plateaus.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start + budget
    N = get_fft_size(n)

    score, b = conv_score(p, n, N)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score

    alpha_current = 0.10
    alpha_candidates = (0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00, 2.00, 4.00)
    additive_candidates = (0.05, 0.20, 0.70, 2.00, 6.00)

    it = 0
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        d, norm = soft_max_gradient(p, b, n, N, beta_factor)
        if norm <= 1e-18:
            no_improve += 50
            if no_improve > 2000:
                break

        improved = False

        # Multiplicative line search.
        if norm > 1e-18:
            du = d / norm
            last_alpha = -1.0
            for alpha in alpha_candidates:
                if abs(alpha - last_alpha) < 1e-12:
                    continue
                last_alpha = alpha
                if abs(alpha - alpha_current) > 1e-12:
                    # Try near the current best alpha first by reordering cheaply.
                    pass
                with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
                    q = p * np.exp(-alpha * du)
                q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
                q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
                s = float(np.sum(q))
                if s <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                q = q / s
                sc, b2 = conv_score(q, n, N)
                if sc + 1e-13 < score:
                    p = q
                    score = sc
                    b = b2
                    improved = True
                    alpha_current = alpha
                    break

        # If multiplicative step failed, try a few additive clipped steps.
        if not improved and norm > 1e-18:
            du = d / norm
            for alpha in additive_candidates:
                q = p - alpha * du
                q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
                q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
                s = float(np.sum(q))
                if s <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                q = q / s
                sc, b2 = conv_score(q, n, N)
                if sc + 1e-13 < score:
                    p = q
                    score = sc
                    b = b2
                    improved = True
                    break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            if score < best_score:
                best = p.copy()
                best_score = score
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 500 == 0:
                # Small random restart around the best point.
                q = block_perturb(best, rng, 0.25)
                sc, b2 = conv_score(q, n, N)
                if sc < best_score:
                    best = q.copy()
                    best_score = sc
                    p = best.copy()
                    score = sc
                    b = b2
                elif no_improve > 6000:
                    break

        it += 1

    return best, best_score


def block_hill_climb(a, n, deadline, seed):
    """Optional block-perturbation hill climb around the current best sequence."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    N = get_fft_size(n)
    score, _ = conv_score(a, n, N)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    sigma = 0.10
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        q = block_perturb(best, rng, sigma)
        sc, _ = conv_score(q, n, N)
        if sc + 1e-13 < best_score:
            best = q.copy()
            best_score = sc
            no_improve = 0
            sigma = max(0.05, 0.92 * sigma)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 200 == 0:
                sigma = min(2.4, sigma * 1.12)
            if no_improve > 12000 and sigma >= 2.0:
                break

    return best, best_score


def final_exact_score(seq):
    """Use the official evaluator, if available."""
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return a list of non-negative heights with low exact evaluate_sequence score.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    hard_deadline = t0 + budget - 25.0

    # Choose lengths.  Larger lengths are empirically better, but evaluation and
    # local search cost grow; 16384 is a robust upper end for this budget.
    if budget >= 1800.0:
        n_list = [2048, 4096, 8192, 16384]
    elif budget >= 750.0:
        n_list = [1024, 2048, 4096, 8192]
    else:
        n_list = [512, 1024, 2048, 4096]

    candidates = []  # list of (fast_score, sequence_array, n)

    # Add official previous construction if present.
    prev = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if prev is not None:
        p = clean_sequence(prev)
        if p.size > 0:
            n = p.size
            N = get_fft_size(n)
            sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
            candidates.append((sc, p, n))

    # Generate diverse candidates.
    for n in n_list:
        base = central_binomial_sequence(n)
        base = clean_sequence(base)
        N = get_fft_size(n)
        sc, _ = conv_score(base, n, N)
        candidates.append((sc, base, n))

        # Reversed central binomial.
        p = clean_sequence(base[::-1])
        sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
        candidates.append((sc, p, n))

        # Two-block ratio construction.
        p = two_block_sequence(n, 0.5, 2.0)
        sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
        candidates.append((sc, p, n))

        # Trend perturbations of the central initializer.
        for strength in (0.15, 0.35, 0.80, -0.15, -0.35):
            p = trend_sequence(base, rng, strength)
            sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
            candidates.append((sc, p, n))

        # Random block perturbations.
        for strength in (0.25, 0.60, 1.40):
            p = block_perturb(base, rng, strength)
            sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
            candidates.append((sc, p, n))

        # A few independent random perturbations from two-block starts.
        base2 = two_block_sequence(n, 0.5, 2.0)
        for strength in (0.35, 1.00):
            p = block_perturb(base2, rng, strength)
            sc, _ = conv_score(p, n, N)
            candidates.append((sc, p, n))

    if not candidates:
        return [1.0]

    # Sort candidates by fast score.
    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])

    best_us = candidates[0][1].copy()
    best_score = candidates[0][0]

    print(f"[start_pool] best fast score={best_score:.10f}, n={best_us.size}, "
          f"candidates={len(candidates)}, elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # Run local gradient search on promising starts.
    # We try the best initial, and a few close competitors if they are not far worse.
    local_used = 0
    max_local_total = max(300.0, budget * 0.55)
    start_indices = []
    for i, (sc, p, n) in enumerate(candidates[:12]):
        if sc <= best_score + 0.12:
            start_indices.append(i)
        if len(start_indices) >= 5:
            break

    if start_indices:
        # Give the best initializer the largest allocation, then decreasing.
        allocs = []
        remaining_alloc = max_local_total
        for rank, idx in enumerate(start_indices):
            if rank == 0:
                a = min(remaining_alloc, 0.50 * max_local_total)
            elif rank == 1:
                a = min(remaining_alloc, 0.25 * max_local_total)
            else:
                a = min(remaining_alloc, 0.12 * max_local_total)
            a = max(60.0, a)
            if remaining_alloc - a < 30.0:
                break
            allocs.append((idx, a))
            remaining_alloc -= a
            local_used += a

        for idx, alloc in allocs:
            if time.monotonic() >= hard_deadline:
                break
            sc, p, n = candidates[idx]
            # Do not spend time on clearly worse starts if time is tight.
            alloc2 = min(alloc, max(30.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - 120.0))
            if alloc2 <= 30.0:
                break
            res_p, res_sc = local_gradient_search(p, n, alloc2, seed=1000 + idx)
            if res_sc < best_score:
                best_score = res_sc
                best_us = res_p.copy()
                print(f"[local] improved to {best_score:.10f}, n={best_us.size}, "
                      f"elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # Final block hill-climb on the best sequence.
    if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline:
        best_us, best_score = block_hill_climb(
            best_us, best_us.size,
            deadline=max(time.monotonic(), hard_deadline - 5.0),
            seed=seed + 777
        )
        print(f"[hillclimb] fast score={best_score:.10f}, n={best_us.size}, "
              f"elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # Exact validation of top candidates.
    exact_candidates = []

    # Always include the current best.
    exact_candidates.append(best_us.copy())

    # Include best initial seeds, especially large-n bases that may be locally hard.
    for sc, p, n in candidates[:8]:
        if sc <= best_score + 0.05:
            exact_candidates.append(p.copy())
        if len(exact_candidates) >= 6:
            break

    # Also include a few largest-n initializers (fast asymptotics may be best).
    by_n = {}
    for sc, p, n in candidates:
        if n not in by_n or sc < by_n[n][0]:
            by_n[n] = (sc, p)
    for n in sorted(by_n.keys(), reverse=True)[:3]:
        _, p = by_n[n]
        exact_candidates.append(p.copy())

    # Remove duplicates by exact tuple-like identity is overkill; just limit count.
    unique_candidates = []
    seen_keys = set()
    for p in exact_candidates:
        if p.size == 0:
            continue
        # Use a coarse key: length and rounded first/last few values as a cheap dedup.
        key = (
            int(p.size),
            float(np.round(p[0], 12)),
            float(np.round(p[p.size // 4], 12)),
            float(np.round(p[3 * p.size // 4], 12)),
            float(np.round(p[-1], 12))
        )
        if key not in seen_keys:
            seen_keys.add(key)
            unique_candidates.append(p)

    best_exact = float("inf")
    best_exact_seq = best_us.copy()

    # Use most of remaining time for exact validation.
    exact_deadline = t0 + budget - 5.0
    for p in unique_candidates:
        if time.monotonic() >= exact_deadline:
            break
        # Very large exact convolutions can be expensive; still try up to 20k.
        if p.size > 25000:
            continue
        try:
            val = final_exact_score(p)
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_exact:
            best_exact = val
            best_exact_seq = p.copy()
            print(f"[exact] n={p.size}, score={val:.10f}, elapsed={time.monotonic()-t0:.2f}s")

    # Safety: ensure official score is finite; if evaluation failed for the chosen
    # sequence, fall back to the first finite exact candidate.
    if not np.isfinite(best_exact) or best_exact_seq.size == 0:
        for p in unique_candidates:
            try:
                val = final_exact_score(p)
            except Exception:
                val = float("inf")
            if np.isfinite(val):
                best_exact = val
                best_exact_seq = p.copy()
                break

    if best_exact_seq.size == 0:
        return [1.0]

    # Final cleanup: non-negative, finite, bounded, Python list.
    out = np.asarray(best_exact_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return [1.0]
    out = out / s
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
